**Local search algorithm ideas:**

Because values can be continuous, we can consider **active lags and gradient step**. Since objective scale invariant, we can fix sum=1. Let c = conv(a,a). Let m = max c. Let active set R = {r: c_r > m - tol}. To decrease m locally, we need choose direction d with ∑d=0, a+d≥0 for small step, and derivative at active lags <0 (at least weighted average). Since derivative c_r' = 2(a*d)_r. We can solve a linear program: maximize -∑_{r∈R} w_r (a*d)_r subject to ∑d=0, ||d||_2≤1, maybe -a/step ≤ d_i ≤∞. This is like least squares. The optimal unconstrained d for weighted sum W = ∑ w_r (a*d)_r is d = -P(B^T w - mean*...)/||...|| where B? (a*d)_r = ∑ a_i d_{r-i} over valid. B is Toeplitz. The objective ∑ w_r c_r'(a) = 2 ∑_i d_i (∑_r w_r a_{r-i}+w_r a_{i-r}?) We can compute derivative of Q(d)=∑ w_r (a*d)_r as linear L^T d. Let L_i = ∑_r w_r a_{r-i} (with conditions) plus ∑_r w_r a_{i-r}? Since (a*d)_r =∑_i a_i d_{r-i}. Sum over r w_r (a*d)_r = ∑_i d_j ∑_r w_r a_{r-j} where j=r-i. Equivalent linear functional with coefficients L_j = (w*a)*_j? full convolution? Specifically L_j = ∑_r w_r a_{r-j} for r-j in [0,n-1]. If w is an array length 2n-1, then L = correlation of w with a: L_j = ∑_r w_r a_{r-j}. This is like full correlation but can compute via convolution: L = np.correlate(w, a)? Use FFT.

To reduce active max, choose d_i proportional to -L_i (project to zero sum) and box. Then take step. This is a projected gradient descent on surrogate F(w,a)=∑_{active}w c_r. We can alternate active set.

Alternatively, minimize m(t)=max_r c_r(a+t d). For given d, choose t to minimize max, then scale.

But gradient may be noisy with many active. Use subgradient power iteration / Frank-Wolfe:
- At current a, active set R (maybe top K lags).
- Find best "vertex" g under linearization? The SOTA LP: maximize ∑g subject to a*g ≤ m. This can be seen as finding farthest point in feasible halfspaces that reduces linear approximation. A simpler method: **Alternating minimization via LP direction + prox step**.

LP direction: Solve min t s.t. a*g ≤ t, g≥0, sum=1. This gives g that minimizes max cross with a. Then update a = (1-α)a + α g. The new M? We can compute exactly and accept if lower. If g == a (a already minimizes cross), maybe stuck. The LP can be solved with active constraints. This is like finding a point in simplex minimizing max of linear functions L_r(g)=a*g_r. It's a **linear programming over simplex**. The optimal g will put mass where marginal? We can solve via scipy for moderate n. This direction may be powerful because it exactly finds best mixing partner for first-order? For small α, derivative of M in direction g-a at active r is 2(a*g_r - a*a_r) ≤0, so M decreases if at least one active improves and g okay. If g is same, active all have L_r= M and L_r(g)=M? Then no improvement possible in this convex relaxation? Could be local optimum.

Can we solve this LP efficiently for n ~1000? Variables n (g) + t. Constraints: 2n-1 inequalities a*g ≤ t, sum g=1, g≥0. The coefficient matrix is convolution Toeplitz. We can use `scipy.optimize.linprog(method='highs')` with dense? For n=1000, constraints 3000, variables 1001, dense matrix 3M entries, memory 24MB per dense, okay. HiGHS fast maybe 0.1s. For n=4096, 12k constraints, 4k vars dense 50M entries, memory 400MB, too high. We can use sparse `scipy.sparse.csr_matrix` and HiGHS supports sparse? linprog highspy with sparse maybe yes. Matrix: constraints for convolution: row r has nonzeros where i+j=r: up to n entries; total nonzeros ~n(2n-1) ~ O(n^2) again! For n=4096, ~33M nonzeros, memory 264MB? Too large. So direct LP dense not for large n. But we can use cutting plane: active constraints only need L_r ≤ t for a subset; but a*g constraints all lags. If m_c ~500 and n=4096, nonzeros ~2M, okay. Then after solving, compute full cross max and add violated constraints. This can work. The LP optimum might have many binding constraints, but cutting plane iterates.

**Local optimization option A: Smooth logsumexp gradient (Adam)**
Define `smooth_loss(a, τ) = logsumexp(c, τ)`? Actually H = τ log(∑ exp(c_r/τ)). Derivative w_r = exp(c_r/τ)/∑. Gradient g = 2*corr(w,a). We minimize H. Scale invariant but we fix sum=1 so H changes. Score approx 2n H? For small τ, H≈M + τ log K. We can update with Adam: maintain m,v of g. Step β (e.g., 1e-3). Update a = a - β * g_hat. Then project to simplex: clip negative to 0, normalize. Because gradient large? We can use multiplicative: a = a * exp(-β g_norm). Then normalize; more stable. But Adam requires sign; multiplicative with normalized gradient may be okay.

However, H gradient descent may spend time plateau. We can combine with **active max line search**: after each step, compute c and M. Accept if M lower (or smooth loss lower with τ?). We can use score if improved.

Potential issue: Since M exact lower bounded by? Gradient on max may have zero gradient at active set if no feasible descent direction? At local min? We can use perturbed random.

**Option B: Pairwise mass transfer / conj grad**:
Select k smallest and largest gradient positions; move mass from high to low. This is natural for simplex. We can use full gradient (weighted) and do a **Frank-Wolfe step** not to vertex but towards a **soft argmin**: target b_i ∝ exp(-γ g_i) (max entropy minimizer of linear approx), then mix a←(1-α)a+α normalize(b). This can be computed via FFT and normalized. For small γ, smooth. To preserve nonneg. This is a variant of mirror descent on simplex with exponential family. We can choose γ so target not too far. Then line search for exact M. This might be effective: target b is the simplex point minimizing ∑g_i b_i plus entropy (regularized linear minimizer). For active gradient, b_i high at low g. It avoids single vertex.

Algorithm iteration:
1. compute c=M, maybe weights w=softmax(c/τ).
2. compute grad g = 2_corr(w,a).
3. Create target b = normalize(exp(-γ * (g - mean(g)))) (maybe subtract mean for sum1). Could include box near zero? If a_i=0, exp maybe small but can become >0; okay.
4. For α in [0.1,0.25,0.5,1] (or line search), candidate = (1-α)a + α b, normalize; compute exact/FFT M; accept best if improved. If no improvement, reduce α/γ and continue or perturb.

This is easy and parallel with search. We can also apply **reweighting** to make target focus on reducing max: choose γ consistent with τ, maybe γ=1/std(g). Or use top/bottom fractions: b_i = normalize(a_i * exp(-γ g_i)) rather than pure exponential, to not move too far from a. That is multiplicative update.

Let's test conceptually at constant a. c triangular. Weighted active near center. grad g_i: for center weights, high at middle. b will boost edges, reduce center, maybe improve. Then new c may have peaks near edges? gradient next moves mass accordingly, seeking flat c. This could converge to balanced c.

Need choose n and τ/γ. Scoring: We can track best exact. Use time.

**Computing gradient with FFT**:
Given a length n, W length v=2n-1. Need b_i = ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} W_{i+j} a_j for i=0..n-1.
This is valid cross-correlation of W and A. We can compute via FFT:
Let L >= v+n-1 = 3n-2? For correlation of W (len v) and a (len n) valid length n. Define A padded L, B = W padded? Standard correlation c_k = ∑_n x[n+k] y[n] for k=0..len(x)-len(y). FFT: if len(x)=v, len(y)=n, correlation = ifft(FFT(x) * conj(FFT(y))) has length L. The valid part is indices? Let's test small. Numpy correlate(x,y,'valid') returns [∑_{n=0}^{m-1} x[n+k] y[n]] for k=0..N-m. For FFT, `np.fft.ifft(np.fft.fft(x, L) * np.fft.conj(np.fft.fft(y, L))).real` gives circular correlation? def fft_convolve_nonneg(a):
    n=len(a); L=np.conn.next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1].clip(0,None)
```

Gradient:
```
def diag_corr(w,a):
    n=len(a); v=2*n-1; L=... >= v+n-1 (3n-2)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(fw * np.conj(fa), L)
    return corr[:n].clip(0,None) # cross-correlation valid
```
Need verify with small. For x=W, y=a, L. corr[k] = ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} W[j+k] a[j]? Let's test mentally n=2, W length3 [w0,w1,w2], a=[a0,a1]. FFT corr with conj(a): result index k: ∑_j? For linear correlation x[k+j] conj(y[j]). If no wrap, k=0: x0 a0 + x1 a1 = w0 a0 + w1 a1. But desired b_i = ∑_j W_{i+j} a_j: i=0: w0 a0 + w1 a1 correct. i=1: w1 a0 + w2 a1. corr index1 should w1 a0 + w2 a1. Good. So return corr[:n]. Need L >= v+n-1 =3n-2. For n=2, L>=4; with power L=4. Good.

**Algorithm inspired by KKT:**

3. Set new a_i ∝ a_i * exp(-η (b_i - const))? Because gradient is b_i.

**Implementation of iterative improver:**
```
def improve_sequence(a, seed, budget_fn/time, tau_factor=0.1, eta=0.3, max_no_improve=...):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a); M=conv_sq(a).max(); best=a.copy(); bestM=M; iters=0; no_impr=0; base_tau=...
    while time<deadline and iters<max:
       c=conv_sq(a); M=c.max()
       if M < bestM: update best
       # weights
       # temperature could adapt: tau = M * tau_factor
       # if M very small? no.
       w = exp((c-M)/tau); w/=sum
       g = gradient_weights(w,a)
       # normalize z
       gm = g.mean(); gs = g.std()
       if gs <= 1e-15: break/stuck -> perturb
       z = (g-gm)/(gs+1e-300)
       # target
       raw = a * exp(-eta*z) # clip exponent
       b = normalize(raw)
       # if b all zero (eta large), fallback
       # line search over alpha and maybe step
       candidates: alpha [1,0.7,0.5,0.3,0.1,0.03]
       for alpha: cand = normalize((1-alpha)*a + alpha*b)
           cM = conv_sq(cand).max()
           if cM < M - 1e-14: accept cand; break
       if no accept:
           # try smaller eta? or coordinate transfer
           direct_mov = ...
           perturb a with noise, no_impr++
       iters+=1
       # every 1000, adapt tau/eta
    return best
```

**Adaptive loop pseudocode:**
```
def local_optimize(a0, deadline, rng, label):
 a=normalize(a0); best=a.copy(); bestM=conv_sq(a).max(); exact maybe
 step=0.5; tau_factor=0.2
 stall=0
 while time < deadline:
   c=conv_sq(a); M=c.max()
   if M < bestM - 1e-15:
       best=a.copy(); bestM=M; stall=0
   else:
       stall+=1
   if stall>limit: perturb a from best or random; step*=0.5? continue
   tau = max(1e-15, tau_factor * (M + c.std()))
   w=softmax(c,tau)
   g=diag_corr(w,a)
   ga=np.dot(a,g)
   scale=np.linalg.norm(g-ga)
   if scale < eps: perturb
   z=(g-ga)/scale
   for eta in [step, min(2,step*2), max(0.01,step*0.5)]:
       exp_arg=np.clip(-eta*z, -10,10)
       raw=a*np.exp(exp_arg)
       b=normalize(raw)
       # maybe add floor
       candM=conv_sq(b).max()
       if candM < M - 1e-15:
           a=b; stall=0; step=min(1.5,step*1.05); break
   else:
       # try multiplicative with alpha not 1: cand = normalize((1-α)*a+α*b) for α...
       # if none, reduce step and perturb
```

**Implement local_optimize more concretely:**
```
def optimize_sequence(a, deadline, rng, verbose=False):
    n = len(a)
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    best = a.copy()
    c = conv_sq(a); c_sum=c.sum(); if c_sum>0: c/=c_sum
    bestM = float(c.max())
    M = bestM
    step = 0.4
    tau_factor = 0.25
    stall = 0
    no_exact = 0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if verbose and it%1000==0: print...
        c = conv_sq(a)
        cs=c.sum()
        if cs>0: c /= cs
        M = float(c.max())
        if M < bestM - 1e-15:
            bestM = M; best=a.copy(); stall=0
            no_exact = (no_exact+1)
        else:
            stall += 1
        # if long time since exact, eval best? optional
        if stall > 100:
            # perturb
            a = random_perturb(best, rng, 0.05 + 0.2*(stall%5))
            step = min(1.0, max(0.05, step*0.7))
            stall = 0
            continue
        tau = max(1e-15, tau_factor * (M + c.std()))
        # c may have small negative? clipped
        w = np.exp((c - M) / tau)
        wsum = w.sum()
        if not finite or wsum<=0: reset w uniform
        w /= wsum
        g = gradient_corr(w, a)
        ga = float(np.dot(a, g))
        z = g - ga
        scale = float(np.linalg.norm(z))
        if scale <= 1e-30:
            a = random_perturb(best, rng,0.1); continue
        z /= scale
        improved=False
        # test aggressive and less
        for eta in (step, max(0.01,0.5*step), min(3.0,2.0*step)):
            arg = np.clip(-eta*z, -12, 12)
            raw = a * np.exp(arg)
            # floor: raw += 1e-15? maybe
            b = normalize_nonneg(raw + 1e-15)
            cn = conv_sq(b)
            csn=cn.sum()
            if csn>0: cn/=csn
            Mn = float(cn.max())
            if Mn < M - 1e-14:
                a = b
                step = min(2.0, step*1.05)
                improved=True
                break
        if not improved:
            # try mixing b with a using alpha<1? We already used b. Maybe if a close, need alpha small.
            # use alpha=0.25 with same b?
            if ... we used b for final, so alpha 1. Could test alpha values:
            for alpha in (0.5,0.25,0.1):
               cand=normalize((1-alpha)*a+alpha*b)
               Mn=...
               if Mn<M: accept
        if not improved:
             step = max(0.02, step*0.8)
             if step <=0.021: perturb
        it +=1
    return best,bestM
```

**Detailed final code skeleton:**
```
"""Search nonnegative step heights by gradient/softmax minimization of max convolution."""
import numpy as np
import time

def _fast_len(l):
    p=1
    while p < l: p <<=1
    return p

def _normalize_nonneg(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    x=np.clip(x,0.0,None)
    s=x.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
       return np.full(x.size,1.0/x.size)
    return x/s

def _fft_conv_sq(a):
    n=a.size; L=_fast_len(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]
    # remove small negative
    out[out<0]=0.0
    s=out.sum()
    if s > 0 and np.isfinite(s):
        out = out / s
    return out

def _fft_grad(w, a):
    # w length 2n-1, a length n
    n=a.size; v=2*n-1; L=_fast_len(v + n -1) # 3n-2
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fw * np.conj(fa), L)[:n]
    out[out<0]=0.0
    # do not normalize
    return out
```
Check `v + n -1 = 3n-2`. For n=1, v=1, L=1? correlation? fine but n large.

Potential issue: `np.fft.rfft` with length L power of two >= req. For grad, L maybe 32768; output corr length L. Valid part first n. Good.

**Random perturb:**
```
def _smooth_noise(n, rng, scale):
    spec = np.fft.rfft(rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n))
    # low-pass keep frequencies below n/4? To avoid high-freq spikes
    mask = np.ones_like(spec); m=int(n//8)+1; mask[m:]=0
    noise = np.fft.irfft(spec*mask, n)
    return scale*noise
```
But creating FFT each perturbation cost. Simpler block: choose random contiguous blocks and scale.
```
def _perturb(a, rng, scale):
    n=len(a); c=a.copy()
    # block multiply
    for _ in range(3):
       width = max(1, int(rng.integers(n//100, n//10)))
       start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
       c[start:start+width] *= np.exp(float(rng.normal(0, scale)))
    # also global small noise
    if scale >0.05: c *= np.exp(float(rng.normal(0, scale*0.1)))
    return _normalize_nonneg(c)
```
Use previous method. For continuous, okay.

```
def _make_starts(parent_arr, n, rng):
    starts=[]
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    # constant
    starts.append(np.full(n,1.0/n))
    # mid functions
    for f, phi in [(1,0.0),(1,1.6),(2,0.0),(2,2.1),(3,0.5),(5,1.0)]:
       x = 0.5 + 0.49*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*i/n + phi)
       starts.append(_normalize_nonneg(x))
    # half-wave antisym
    half = 0.5 + 0.49*np.cos(np.pi*(i+0.5)/n)
    starts.append(_normalize_nonneg(half))
    # random lognormal smooth
    y = rng.normal(0,1,n)
    y = np.convolve(y, np.ones(8)/8, mode='same')
    starts.append(_normalize_nonneg(np.exp(0.5*y)))
    # random binary dense
    b = (rng.random(n) < 0.5).astype(float)
    if b.sum()>0: starts.append(_normalize_nonnorm)
    # random antisymmetric binary/continuous
    u = rng.normal(0,1,n//2)
    a = np.empty(n); a[:n//2]=u; a[n//2:] = -u if n even? Need reverse.
    a[n//2:] = -u[::-1] if n//2 else ...
    starts.append(_normalize_nonneg(1 + 0.7*a))
    # parent
```

       p = np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim !=1: return None

    if not np.isfinite(p).all(): return None

**Optimize function details more robust:**
Use parameters adaptive:
```
def optimize_sequence(a0, deadline, rng, tag=''):
    a = _normalize_nonneg(np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64))
    n = a.size
    c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
    M = float(c.max())
    best_score = 2.0*n*M
    best = a.copy()
    best_M = M
    step = 0.4
    tau_factor = 0.2
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() - last_print > 10:
            print(f"  {tag} it={it} score_est={best_score:.6f}")
            last_print = time.monotonic()
        c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
        M = float(c.max())
        score = 2.0*n*M
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score=score; best=a.copy(); best_M=M; stall=0
        else:
            stall += 1
        if stall > 300:
            # restart from best with perturb
            a = _perturb(best, rng, 0.1)
            # If still no improve after perturb?
            step = min(1.0, max(0.1, step*0.7))
            stall = 0
            continue
        # weights
        # If M is 0? (n? no) ; use std
        c_std = float(c.std())
        tau = max(1e-15, tau_factor * (M + c_std))
        # If tau very small, weights top active; exp overflow not because c-M <=0, so okay.
        exponent = (c - M) / tau
        # exponent <=0; min maybe -large. Clip lower to -50 to keep sum numeric.
        exponent = np.maximum(exponent, -50.0)
        w = np.exp(exponent)
        ws = w.sum()
        if ws <=0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
            w = np.full_like(c, 1.0/c.size)
        else:
            w /= ws
        g = _fft_grad(w, a)
        ga = float(np.dot(a,g))
        z = g - ga
        scale = float(np.linalg.norm(z))
        if scale < 1e-30:
            a = _perturb(best, rng, 0.1)
            step = max(0.05, step*0.7)
            stall +=1
            continue
        z /= scale
        improved=False
        # Candidate b for several eta
        # Use current a for all
        cands=[]
        for eta in (step, max(0.02, 0.5*step), min(2.5, 2.0*step)):
            arg = -eta*z
            arg = np.clip(arg, -12.0, 12.0)
            raw = a * np.exp(arg)
            raw += 1e-16
            b = _normalize_nonneg(raw)
            if b is same as a? check max diff; if no change skip
            cn = _fft_conv_sq(b)
            M_n = float(cn.max())
            sc_n = 2.0*n*M_n
            if sc_n < score - 1e-12:
                a = b
                improved=True
                step = min(2.0, step*1.02)
                break
            cands.append((sc_n, b))
        if not improved:
            # Try alpha blends with candidates from least aggressive? sort by sc_n
            # cands maybe 3. Try alpha=0.5,0.25 for best cand
            if cands:
                cands.sort(key=lambda t: t[0]) # no lambda allowed! Need helper function? Can't use lambda. We'll avoid sorting by manual or use cands[0].
```

def exact_score(seq_list):
    try:
       val = float(evaluate_sequence(seq_list))
       return val if np.isfinite(val) else np.inf
    except Exception:
       return np.inf

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=400, **kwargs):
    total_budget = float(budget_s)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, total_budget - 5.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n_search = 8192 if total_budget > 120 else 4096
    starts = _make_starts(n_search, rng)
    scored = []
    for a in starts:
       an, sc = _score_and_normalize(a)
       if np.isfinite(sc): scored.append((sc, an))
    # If no scored, uniform
    scored.sort(key=_score_pair_key)
    best_seq = scored[0][1] if scored else np.ones(n_search)/n_search; best_est=...
    final_candidates=[best_seq]
    weights = [0.40,0.25,0.15,0.10,0.05]
    now=time.monotonic()
    total_time=deadline-now
    for rank, item in enumerate(scored[:min(5,len(scored))]):
       t = total_time * weights[rank]
       if t < 5 or time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       dl = time.monotonic() + min(t, deadline-time.monotonic())
       seq, sc = optimize_sequence(item[1], dl, rng, tag=f's{rank}')
       final_candidates.append(seq)
       if sc < best_est:
          best_seq=seq; best_est=sc
    # continue best with remaining
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
       seq, sc = optimize_sequence(best_seq, deadline, rng, tag='final')
       if sc < best_est: best_seq=seq; best_est=sc
       final_candidates.append(seq)
    # exact selection
    return _choose_by_exact_scaling(final_candidates, deadline- maybe)
```

**Implement `_make_starts`:**
```
def _make_starts(n, rng):
    starts=[]
    parent=_get_parent()
    if parent is not None:
       prio...
       starts.append((parent, 0))
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    starts.append((np.ones(n)/n,1))
    # For f 1..7, two phases
    for f in (1,2,3,4,5,7):
       for phi in (0.0, np.pi*(f//2)):
          vals = 0.5 + 0.48*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*i/n + phi)
          a = _normalize_nonneg(vals)
          starts.append((a,2))
       # sine
       vals = 0.5 + 0.48*np.sin(2*np.pi*f*i/n + phi)
       ...
    # half-wave antisym
    vals = 0.5 + 0.48*np.cos(np.pi*(i+0.5)/n)
    starts.append...
    # long low-frequency random: use rng.normal and smooth via several cos?
    y = rng.normal(0,1,n); y = _smooth_array(y, 8) # top-level helper using convolution
    starts.append((normalize(exp(0.4*y)),3))
    # random binary with p=0.5
    b = (rng.random(n)<0.5).astype(float); if sum>100: starts.append(normalize,4)
    # sparse-ish random p=0.3,0.7 maybe
    for p in (0.3,0.7): ...
    # high/low alternating sinus phase
    vals=0.5+0.48*np.cos(2*np.pi*n//2*i/n) # alternating
```

**Smooth helper using convolution can be top-level:**
```
def _smooth_array(x, width):
    if width <=1: return x
    kernel = np.ones(width)/width
    # use np.convolve direct O(n*width) if width small; or FFT
    return np.convolve(x, kernel, mode='same')
```
For n=8192 width 16, fine. But no need.

**Random binary normalization:** If binary has zeros, internal gradient target `a*exp` with zero a will keep zeros unless floor. We add floor in candidate, so zeros can reappear. But if initial all zero? no.

Pseudo final code:
```
"""..."""
import numpy as np
import time

def _fast_len(target):
    p=1
    while p < target:
       p <<= 1
    return p

def _normalize_nonneg(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
       return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x[x < 0.0] = 0.0
    s = float(x.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       return np.ones_like(x) / float(x.size)
    return x / s

def _fft_conv_sq(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0: return np.array([])
    L = _fast_len(2*n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)
    out = out[:2*n - 1]
    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=np.float64)
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    s = float(out.sum())
    if np.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
       out = out / s
    return out

def _fft_grad(w, a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0: return np.array([])
    v = 2*n - 1
    L = _fast_len(v + n - 1)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fw * np.conj(fa), L)
    out = out[:n]
    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=np.float64)
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out

def _estimate_score(a):
    a = _normalize_nonneg(a)
    c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
    if c.size == 0: return a, np.inf
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m): return a, np.inf
    return a, 2.0 * float(a.size) * m

def _perturb(a, rng, scale):
    n = a.size
    c = a.copy()
    if n > 0 and scale > 0:
       # up to 3 blocks
       min_w = max(1, n // 200)
       max_w = max(min_w + 1, n // 8)
       for _ in range(3):
           width = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w - min_w + 1)) maybe invalid if
```
`rng.integers(low, high)` high exclusive. Use:
```
lo = max(1, n//200)
hi = max(lo+1, n//10)
width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi+1))
start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
c[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, scale)))
```
If `scale` too large, exp maybe exp(5) okay; normal sigma scale maybe 0.1-1. If scale=1, exp normal can be e^3=20, okay. Use scale in `_perturb` as sigma; we pass 0.1-0.3.
Also global noise:
```
 if scale > 0.05:
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, scale*0.25, n)
    c *= np.exp(noise)
```
Then normalize.

But `_perturb(best, rng, 0.08 + 0.2*((stall % 5)*0.05))` scale maybe 0.18. Good.

**`_make_starts`** with start length n. Need parent priority. We'll return list of `(a, priority)`.

```
def _get_parent():
    try:
       p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
       return None
    if p.ndim != 1:
       p = p.ravel()
    if p.size < 64 or p.size > 15000:
       return None
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = p.sum()
    if s <= 0: return None
    return p / s
```
Priority: if parent, priority 0. Others 1+rank. To sort ties, lower priority.

Starts list can contain parent multiple times? We can check if already by score? Not needed.

```
def _make_starts(n, rng):
    starts=[]
    parent=_get_parent()
    if parent is not None:
       starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(parent), 0))
    uni=np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64); starts.append((uni, 1))
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    prio=2
    for f in (1,2,3,5,7):
       # avoid too many if n large? okay
       for phi in (0.0, 1.0, 2.0):
          vals=0.5 + 0.48*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*i/float(n)+phi)
          starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(vals), prio))
          vals=0.5 + 0.48*np.sin(2*np.pi*f*i/float(n)+phi)
          starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(vals), prio))
       prio+=1
    # half-wave anti
    vals=0.5+0.48*np.cos(np.pi*(i+0.5)/float(n))
    starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(vals), 10))
    # random low-freq
    y = rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
    # smooth with moving average width 11
    k=11; kernel=np.ones(k)/k; y=np.convolve(y,kernel,mode='same')
    starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(np.exp(0.4*y)), 12))
    # random binary densities
    for p in (0.35,0.5,0.65):
       b=(rng.random(n)<p).astype(np.float64)
       if b.sum()>n*0.05:
          starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(b),13))
    # random asymmetric continuous
    u=rng.normal(0,1,n//2)
    a=np.empty(n); m=n//2
    a[:m]=u; a[m:] = -u[::-1]
    if n%2: a[-1]=0.0
    vals=1.0 + 0.7*a
    starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(vals),14))
    return starts
```

"""..."""
import numpy as np
import time

def _fast_len(target):
    target = int(target)
    p = 1
    while p < target:
        p <<= 1
    return p

def _normalize_nonneg(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.ndim != 1:
       x = x.ravel()
    if x.size == 0:
       return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       return np.ones(x.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(x.size)
    return x / s
...
```

```
def _perturb(a, rng, scale):
    a = _normalize_nonneg(a)
    n = a.size
    c = a.copy()
    theta = max(0.0, min(2.0, float(scale)))
    if theta <= 0 or n ==0:
       return c
    lo = max(1, min(n, n//200))
    hi = max(lo, min(n, n//10))
    for _ in range(4):
       if n == 0: break
       width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi+1)) if hi >= lo else lo
       if width > n: width=n
       start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1)) if n >= width else 0
       c[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, theta)))
    # global low small
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.5*theta, n)
    c = c * np.exp(noise)
    return _normalize_nonneg(c)
```
If n=1, lo=1, hi=1, width=1, start=0. good. `c[start:start+width]` for start int.
`theta` capped 2; exp(normal(0,2)) could be up to e^6=400, okay. `noise` exp up to e^3=20.

In `_make_starts`, random smooth:
```
def _smooth_array(x, width):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    if x.size < width: return x
    ker=np.ones(width,dtype=float)/float(width)
    return np.convolve(x, ker, mode='same')
```

**Exact function:**
```
def _exact_score_list(lst):
    try:
       val = evaluate_sequence(lst)
       val = float(val)
    except Exception:
       return np.inf
    return val if np.isfinite(val) else np.inf
```
Assumes evaluate_sequence global. If `evaluate_sequence` not defined, NameError caught? NameError is Exception, return inf. But then final fallback.

**Scaling final:**
```
def _choose_exact(candidates, exact_deadline):
    best_score = np.inf; best_list = None
    # unique by length maybe
    seen=[]
    uniq=[]
    for c in candidates:
       a=_normalize_nonneg(c)
       # if a.size <1 skip
       # if same size and allclose to a previous? skip maybe expensive
       uniq.append(a)
       if len(uniq)>=5: break
    scales = (0.02, 0.1, 0.5, 2.0, 8.0, 40.0, 200.0, 1000.0)
    for a in uniq:
       if a.size ==0: continue
       max_a=float(a.max()); sum_a=float(a.sum())
       if max_a <=0: max_a=1e-300
       for s in scales:
           if time.monotonic() >= exact_deadline:
               break
           if sum_a * s < 0.011:
               continue
           if max_a * s > 1000.0 * (1.0 + 1e-12):
               continue
           out = a * float(s)
           # If max after rounding >1000, cap? evaluate caps but sum changes; safer skip if >1000
           if not out.size:
              continue
           if float(out.max()) > 1000.0:
              continue
           lst = [float(x) for x in out]
           sc = _exact_score_list(lst)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
              best_score=sc; best_list=lst
       if time.monotonic() >= exact_deadline: break
    if best_list is None:
       best_list=[1.0]
       best_score=_exact_score_list(best_list)
    print("Final exact score estimate:", best_score)
    return best_list
```

        best_score = sc; best_seq = seq
```
- Then if time left: `if time.monotonic() < search_deadline - 1.0: seq, sc = optimize_sequence(best_seq.copy(), search_deadline, rng, "final") ...`
- Final exact: `exact_deadline = started + max(1.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)`; maybe if exact_deadline < time.monotonic(), set to time+0.1. `return _choose_by_exact(candidates, exact_deadline)`.

Need ensure candidates are numpy arrays or lists. `_choose_by_exact` does `_normalize_nonneg` which works on arrays or lists.

Potential circular global `height_sequence_1` catch. Good.

Let's consider if final candidate arrays are copies? optimize best is copy maybe. If we append same object and later modify? Best not modified after return? optimize returns best, internal `a` may be modified after returning? No. But if best_seq object is also used in final optimize and then modified? optimize takes copy? In call we pass `best_seq.copy()`, original unchanged. Candidate array stored is returned `seq`; internal optimize may later? No after return not. In final exact, if we keep candidates list with best and then normalize, it creates new array. fine.

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative coefficient sequence minimizing

    2 * len(a) * max(convolution(a, a)) / (sum(a))^2

using FFT-based smooth surrogate gradients, adaptive step sizes, multiple
start points, and a final exact-evaluator-based scale selection.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _fast_len(target):
    """Smallest power of two >= target."""
    target = int(target)
    p = 1
    while p < target:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _normalize_nonneg(x):
    """Clip to nonnegative, replace nonfinite by zero, and normalize sum to 1."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.ndim != 1:
        x = x.ravel()
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)

    s = float(x.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(x.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(x.size)

    return x / s


def _fft_conv_sq(a):
    """
    Full convolution a * a using FFT.

    The result is clipped to nonnegative and rescaled so that its sum is 1.
    This is useful because the objective is scale-invariant and the search
    keeps sum(a) = 1.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    L = _fast_len(2 * n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)
    out = out[:2 * n - 1]
    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=np.float64)

    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0

    s = float(out.sum())
    if np.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
        out = out / s

    return out


def _fft_grad(w, a):
    """
    For convolution weights w = (w_0,...,w_{2n-2}) and a of length n,
    return b_i = sum_j w_{i+j} a_j, i = 0,...,n-1.

    This is the gradient of sum_r w_r (a*a)_r -> 1/2, aside from the factor 2.
    Computed by valid linear cross-correlation w<--a via FFT.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    v = 2 * n - 1
    L = _fast_len(v + n - 1)

    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)

    out = np.fft.irfft(fw * np.conj(fa), L)
    out = out[:n]
    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=np.float64)

    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def _estimate_score(a):
    """FFT-based estimate of the exact evaluator under sum(a)=1 normalization."""
    a = _normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a, np.inf

    c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return a, np.inf

    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return a, np.inf

    return a, 2.0 * float(a.size) * m


def _smooth_array(x, width):
    """Simple moving-average smoothing."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
        return x

    width = int(width)
    if width <= 1 or x.size < width:
        return x

    kernel = np.ones(width, dtype=np.float64) / float(width)
    return np.convolve(x, kernel, mode="same")


def _get_parent():
    """Use the global height_sequence_1 if it is a valid finite 1-D sequence."""
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None

    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.ravel()

    if p.size < 64 or p.size > 15000:
        return None

    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)

    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None

    return p / s


def _make_starts(n, rng):
    """Create a diverse set of starting sequences."""
    if n < 8:
        n = 8

    starts = []
    parent = _get_parent()
    if parent is not None:
        starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(parent), 0))

    # Constant sequence.
    starts.append((np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), 1))

    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    # Antisymmetric half-wave.
    half = 0.5 + 0.48 * np.cos(np.pi * (i + 0.5) / float(n))
    starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(half), 2))

    # Low-frequency smooth random.
    y = _smooth_array(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n), 11)
    starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(np.exp(0.35 * y)), 3))

    # Dense random binary starts.
    for p in (0.35, 0.5, 0.65):
        b = (rng.random(n) < p).astype(np.float64)
        if b.sum() > max(16, 0.05 * n):
            starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(b), 4))

    # Random antisymmetric continuous pattern.
    m = n // 2
    u = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, m)
    asym = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    asym[:m] = u
    if n % 2 == 0:
        asym[m:] = -u[::-1]
    else:
        asym[m + 1:] = -u[::-1]
        asym[m] = 0.0
    starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(1.0 + 0.7 * asym), 5))

    # A few sinusoidal starts.
    prio = 6
    for f in (1, 2, 3):
        for phi in (0.0, 1.3, 2.7):
            vals = 0.5 + 0.47 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f * i / float(n) + phi)
            starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(vals), prio))

            vals = 0.5 + 0.47 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f * i / float(n) + phi)
            starts.append((_normalize_nonneg(vals), prio))

    return starts


def _perturb(a, rng, scale):
    """Block multiplicative perturbation and mild global noise."""
    a = _normalize_nonneg(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a

    c = a.copy()
    theta = max(0.0, min(1.5, float(scale)))
    if theta <= 0.0:
        return c

    lo = max(1, min(n, n // 200))
    hi = max(lo, min(n, n // 10))

    for _ in range(4):
        width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
        if width > n:
            width = n
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
        c[start:start + width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, theta)))

    noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.5 * theta, n)
    c = c * np.exp(noise)

    return _normalize_nonneg(c)


def optimize_sequence(a0, deadline, rng, tag=""):
    """
    Local search for one initial sequence.

    At each iteration:
      1. compute c = a*a and current score,
      2. build softmax weights over high convolution coefficients,
      3. compute the corresponding gradient,
      4. project a multiplicative gradient step back to the simplex,
      5. line-search a few step sizes by evaluating the FFT score.
    """
    a = _normalize_nonneg(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64), np.inf

    c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
    M = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    best_M = M
    best_score = 2.0 * float(n) * best_M
    best = a.copy()

    step = 0.5
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # Refresh current score and update best.
        cur_score = 2.0 * float(n) * M

        if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
            a = best.copy()
            c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
            M = float(c.max())
            cur_score = 2.0 * float(n) * M

        if np.isfinite(cur_score) and cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cur_score
            best = a.copy()
            best_M = M
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1

        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 12.0:
            print(f"    {tag}: iter={it}, best_score={best_score:.9f}")
            last_print = now

        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        # If stuck, try escaping from the best point.
        if stall > 250:
            a = _perturb(best, rng, 0.10 + 0.10 * float((stall % 5)))
            c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
            M = float(c.max())
            step = max(0.2, min(1.0, step * 0.8))
            stall = 0
            it += 1
            continue

        # Construct weights.  Usually softmax over current coefficients.  When
        # stuck, use a harder, active-coefficient-like mask.
        cs = float(c.std()) if c.size else 0.0
        if stall > 120:
            thresh = M - max(1e-15, 0.12 * M)
            w = (c >= thresh).astype(np.float64)
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if ws <= 1.0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
                exponent = (c - M) / max(1e-15, 0.25 * M)
                exponent = np.clip(exponent, -50.0, 0.0)
                w = np.exp(exponent)
                ws = float(w.sum())
                if ws <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
                    w = np.full(c.size, 1.0 / float(max(1, c.size)), dtype=np.float64)
                else:
                    w = w / ws
            else:
                w = w / ws
        else:
            tau = max(1e-15, 0.25 * M + 0.05 * cs)
            exponent = (c - M) / tau
            exponent = np.clip(exponent, -50.0, 0.0)
            w = np.exp(exponent)
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if ws <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
                w = np.full(c.size, 1.0 / float(max(1, c.size)), dtype=np.float64)
            else:
                w = w / ws

        # Gradient of the smooth weighted objective.
        g = _fft_grad(w, a)
        ga = float(np.dot(a, g))
        z = g - ga
        zn = float(np.linalg.norm(z))

        if not np.isfinite(zn) or zn <= 1e-30:
            a = _perturb(best, rng, 0.12)
            c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
            M = float(c.max())
            step = max(0.1, step * 0.75)
            it += 1
            continue

        z = z / zn

        improved = False
        stored = []

        # Try several clean exponential updates.
        eta_values = (step, max(0.02, 0.5 * step), min(2.2, 2.0 * step))
        for eta in eta_values:
            arg = -eta * z
            arg = np.clip(arg, -12.0, 12.0)

            raw = a * np.exp(arg)
            raw += 1e-16
            b = _normalize_nonneg(raw)

            if float(np.max(np.abs(b - a))) < 1e-16:
                continue

            cb = _fft_conv_sq(b)
            Mb = float(cb.max())
            sb = 2.0 * float(n) * Mb

            stored.append((b, sb))

            if sb < cur_score - 1e-12:
                a = b
                M = Mb
                if sb < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score = sb
                    best = a.copy()
                    best_M = M
                improved = True
                step = min(2.0, step * 1.03)
                stall = 0
                break

        # If direct exponential moves are not accepted, try blends with the
        # best one among those candidates.
        if not improved and stored:
            idx = 0
            for j in range(1, len(stored)):
                if stored[j][1] < stored[idx][1]:
                    idx = j
            bb = stored[idx][0]

            for alpha in (0.5, 0.25, 0.1):
                cand = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * bb
                cand = _normalize_nonneg(cand)
                cc = _fft_conv_sq(cand)
                Mc = float(cc.max())
                sc = 2.0 * float(n) * Mc

                if sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
                    a = cand
                    M = Mc
                    if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best_score = sc
                        best = a.copy()
                        best_M = M
                    improved = True
                    break

        if not improved:
            step = max(0.03, step * 0.85)
            if step <= 0.035 and stall > 30:
                a = _perturb(best, rng, 0.08 + 0.18 * float((stall % 4)))
                c = _fft_conv_sq(a)
                M = float(c.max())
                step = 0.5
                stall = 0

        it += 1

    if best.score if hasattr(best, "score") else True:
        pass

    return best, best_score


def _start_key(item):
    """Sort key for (score, priority, array)."""
    return (item[0], item[1])


def _exact_score_list(lst):
    """Safely call the supplied exact evaluator."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(lst)
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf

    return val if np.isfinite(val) else np.inf


def _choose_by_exact(candidates, deadline):
    """
    Choose the final returned vector by testing a few scale factors with the
    exact evaluator.  The objective is scale-invariant in exact arithmetic,
    but small floating-point differences can matter.
    """
    best_score = np.inf
    best_list = None

    # Keep a few distinct candidates.
    uniq = []
    for c in candidates:
        a = _normalize_nonneg(c)
        if a.size == 0:
            continue
        uniq.append(a)
        if len(uniq) >= 4:
            break

    scales = (0.02, 0.1, 0.5, 2.0, 8.0, 40.0, 200.0, 1000.0)

    for a in uniq:
        if a.size == 0:
            continue

        max_a = float(a.max())
        sum_a = float(a.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(max_a) or max_a <= 0.0:
            max_a = 1e-300
        if not np.isfinite(sum_a) or sum_a <= 0.0:
            continue

        for s in scales:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break

            if sum_a * s < 0.011:
                continue
            if max_a * s > 1000.0 * (1.0 + 1e-12):
                continue

            out = a * float(s)
            if out.size == 0:
                continue
            if float(out.max()) > 1000.0:
                continue

            lst = [float(x) for x in out]
            sc = _exact_score_list(lst)

            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_list = lst

        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

    if best_list is None:
        best_list = [1.0]
        best_score = _exact_score_list(best_list)

    print("Final exact score:", best_score)
    return best_list


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point.

    Runs a multi-start FFT gradient search and returns the best sequence found
    before the time budget, after final exact-evaluator-based scale selection.
    """
    started = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(10.0, float(budget_s))

    # Leave time for exact final selection.
    search_deadline = started + max(1.0, total_budget - 12.0)
    exact_deadline = started + max(1.0, total_budget - 2.0)

    if total_budget >= 250.0:
        n_search = 8192
    elif total_budget >= 100.0:
        n_search = 4096
    else:
        n_search = 2048

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    starts = _make_starts(n_search, rng)

    scored = []
    for a, prio in starts:
        an, sc = _estimate_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, prio, an))

    if not scored:
        return [1.0]

    scored.sort(key=_start_key)

    best_seq = scored[0][2].copy()
    best_score = scored[0][0]
    candidates = [best_seq]

    print("Initial estimated scores:")
    for sc, prio, _ in scored[:8]:
        print(f"  prio={prio}, est_score={sc:.9f}")

    weights = (0.35, 0.25, 0.15, 0.10, 0.05)
    total_work = max(1.0, search_deadline - time.monotonic())

    for rank in range(min(5, len(scored))):
        if time.monotonic() >= search_deadline:
            break

        t = total_work * weights[rank]
        if rank > 0 and t < 5.0:
            break

        dl = time.monotonic() + min(t, search_deadline - time.monotonic())
        if dl <= time.monotonic():
            break

        print(f"Optimizing start {rank}, est_score={scored[rank][0]:.9f}")
        seq, sc = optimize_sequence(scored[rank][2].copy(), dl, rng, f"start{rank}")
        candidates.append(seq)

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()

    # Continue polishing the global best for any remaining search time.
    if time.monotonic() < search_deadline - 1.5:
        seq, sc = optimize_sequence(best_seq.copy(), search_deadline, rng, "final")
        candidates.append(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()

    # Make sure the very best estimated sequence is among candidates examined
    # exactly, even if some exact early calls already chose another scaled form.
    candidates.append(best_seq)

    return _choose_by_exact(candidates, exact_deadline)
```
